Could update corr arrays incrementally per flip O(n) for all d? To know max, maintain counts c_pos[d], c_neg[d] and a max-heap or counters. Flip changes c_pos[d] for d=1..n-1: if turning 0->1 at p:
- positive first factor: for all d with p+d<n, term h_p(1-h_{p+d}) gains (1 - h_{p+d}).
- positive second factor: for d with p-d>=0, term h_{p-d}(1-h_p): previously h_p old maybe affects second: old h_p=0 so term 0; new h_p=1 term h_{p-d}*0=0? Wait if flip 0->1 at p as second: (1-h_p) changes from 1 to 0, so P_d loses h_{p-d}. So delta_pos[d] = (p+d<n ? (1 - h[p+d]) : 0) - (p-d>=0 ? h[p-d] : 0). Similarly negative Q: Q_d = sum_{i=d}^{n-1} h_i(1-h_{i-d}). Flip p:
- as first i=p: for d with p-d>=0, gain (1-h[p-d])
- as second j=p: for d with p+d<n, previous term h[p+d](1-h[p]) old= h[p+d] (since h_p=0, 1-h=1); new 0, so lose h[p+d]. delta_neg[d] = (p-d>=0 ? 1-h[p-d] :0) - (p+d<n ? h[p+d] :0). Nice.

According to numpy: correlate(a,v) = sum_n a_{n+k} conj(v_n), for k where? full: output[c] = sum_n a_{n + (c - (M-1))} v_n? Let's test small: a=[a0,a1], v=[v0,v1]. full length3. So pos=P, neg=N.

Our local arrays pos[d] P_d = ∑_{i=0}^{n-d-1} h_i(1-h_{i+d}); neg[d] Q_d = ∑_{i=d}^{n-1} h_i(1-h_{i-d}) = ∑_{j=0}^{n-d-1} h_{j+d}(1-h_j). Yes.

For flip 0->1 at p, update pos as above. Let's verify delta_pos for p toggle. pos[d] = ∑ i=0..n-d-1 a_i b_{i+d}. If a_p toggled. If p is first index i=p: term a_p b_{p+d}, gain if p≤n-d-1 (d≤n-1-p): delta1 = b_{p+d} - old? For 0->1: b=1-a_{p+d}, old term 0, new b. For 1->0: delta1=-b. For 0->1: s=1, matches earlier (1-next)-prev. For 1->0: s=-1.
Neg Q_d = ∑_{i=0}^{n-d-1} a_{i+d}(1-a_i). Toggle p as second (index i+d=p, i=p-d): term a_p b_{p-d}: delta1 = s*b_{p-d} if p-d≥0. As first (index i=p, i+d=p+d? - p = j+d (first factor) => j=p-d≥0: delta s*b_{p-d}.
- p = j (second factor) => j=p, d≤n-1-p: term a_{p+d} b_p, delta s? b_p changes by s, a_{p+d} factor, old b; delta = s? For 0->1, b from1 to0, delta = -a_{p+d}. For 1->0, delta=+a_{p+d}. So delta = -s*a_{p+d}. Wait sign: if s=+1, b decreases by1, delta=-a; if s=-1, b increases, delta=+a = -s*a? since -(-1)=+1 yes. So delta_neg = s*(1-a_{p-d}) - s*a_{p+d}. Similar but swapped? Let's compare earlier: for 0->1, delta_neg=(1-prev)-next, same as pos? Wait earlier for neg: as first i=p gain (1-h[p-d]) for d where p-d>=0, as second j=p lose h[p+d]. That gives (1-prev)-next, same as pos! Let's re-evaluate: Q_d = ∑_{i=d}^{n-1} a_i(1-a_{i-d}). If p is first i=p: term a_p(1-a_{p-d}), delta = s*(1-a_{p-d}). They differ: pos: (1-next)-prev, neg: (1-prev)-next. For 0->1. Yes. Need correct.

Binary local search improvement:
Given pos,neg arrays, current max M. Flips can improve if reduce max or distribute. Simple steepest descent: for each candidate p, compute deltas, find new max over pos+delta_pos and neg+delta_neg. Choose if < current M (or maybe accept equal with lower secondary sum). Update full arrays. Repeat until no improvement or time. Computing all candidates naively O(n^2) per iteration? For n=180, one pass O32k, fine. But repeated many: maybe okay. But for n=720, pass O518k, okay; 100 passes =50M, fine. But each candidate delta compute involves slicing copies O(n) Python, total O(n^2) with Python overhead high. We can vectorize candidate evaluation all at once? Let's explore.
For each candidate p, delta_pos[d] = s*((1-h[p+d]) - h[p-d]) with boundaries. This is like for all p,d, but matrix n x n, too memory for n=2000. For n=180, n^2=32k, okay. Could build full delta matrix for 0->1 candidates and 1->0? For each p, deltas depend on h. We need new max for each p. Could compute for all p simultaneously using broadcasting if n small: h shape (n,1), j? For d axis, p+d, p-d. Use indices. n=360 matrix 129k ok. n=720 matrix 518k ok. n=1440 matrix 2M maybe ok. But evaluating both pos/neg, max with current arrays. We can do vectorized: p_array (c,1), d_array (1,n-1). p+d valid mask. prev = h[p-d] where valid, next = h[p+d]. For 0->1, delta_pos = (1-next - prev)*valid masks? Need handle invalid with 0. For all p, compute delta_pos matrix (c,n-1), delta_neg, then new_pos = pos[None,:] + delta_pos, new_neg = neg + delta_neg; max_per_p = max(new_pos.max(axis=1), new_neg.max(axis=1)). Choose p with least max. For c up to n, memory ~ n*n ints. If n=360 (129k) small. If n=1440, 2M ints 16MB each, multiple arrays 100MB maybe okay but time. But doing this full pass many times might be heavy. We can choose lower n for binary (n=360 or 512) for full steepest. For n=180, easy.

But if we use steepest descent with full matrix, after each improvement we need recompute? We can precompute for current h all delta matrices each pass O(n^2). Updating h changes deltas. We can compute ∇c_l for each l efficiently via correlations of basis? Observe c vector = R(h, 1-h). Let a=h, b=1-h. For a fixed weight vector w over lags length 2n-1, F = w · correlate(a,b) = ∑_{l} w_l ∑_i a_{i+...} b_{i+...}. This is a bilinear form F = a^T K w b? Gradient w.r.t a: ∑_l w_l (correlation of b with weights properly shifted), gradient w.r.t b similarly. Since b=1-h = 1 - h, gradient wrt h from a and b: ∂F/∂h = grad_a - grad_b (because b = 1-h, derivative of F wrt h via a + dF/db * (-1)). We can compute grad_a and grad_b by convolutions of w with b/a.

Better: Use two-swap but choose one coordinate at a time using heuristic: select p zero and q one independently optimizing a surrogate. For a pair, new max = max over d pos[d] + a_p[d] + b_q[d]. We can do a ruin-and-create: choose random q one to remove, find best p zero to add (single insertion) that minimizes max after both? We can first apply removal (h_q=0, sum decreases), then find best p to add (sum restore) minimizing max from that intermediate. This two-step move is exact for the sequence of flips: first remove q, then add p. The final state after both can be achieved; the path sum -1 then restore. We can evaluate intermediate and final. Pair cost computed exactly if we update after first and then test p. Algorithm:
- Current h balanced, pos,neg arrays.
- For each possible q (1)? For each q, simulate removal 1->0: update pos,neg (or just compute arrays pos_r, neg_r). Now need choose p zero (p≠q, h_p=0) to add that minimizes final max of pos_r + add_p, neg_r + add_p. This is a best insertion problem (single flip 0->1) from an unbalanced state with sum target-1. We can compute for all candidate p maybe vectorized O(n^2) using delta_add matrix relative to removed state. But after removal, h changed at q, affecting insertion deltas for p (if p near q) with only local corrections. We can precompute insertion delta matrix for current h with sum target? Then remove q and correct? Let's derive more efficient.

For a given removed state (h without q), insertion delta for p is based on h with q=0. If current h has q=1, the delta computed with current h may be slightly wrong for p ± d where neighbor q: for d=|p-q|, prev/next includes q. Detailed binary pair steepest function:

Function compute_pos_neg_binary(h):

Function gradients:
```
def smooth_max_obj(x, tau):
   c = full corr
   m = np.max(c)
   # avoid overflow: exp((c-m)/tau) if tau>0
   e = np.exp((c-m)/tau)
   s = e.sum()
   return float((m + tau*np.log(s)) * dx)

def smooth_max_grad(x, tau):
   c = full corr
   m = np.max(c)
   e = np.exp((c-m)/tau) / e.sum()
   # w length 2n-1, nonnegative sum1
   # build u,v
   u = w[::-1][:n]? Wait mapping.
```

Now binary local search implementation details.

Function `fix_sum_binary(h, target)`? For binary ensure sum int. Given float h, if need target: create int candidate by threshold/sort.
`binary_from_float(x, target)`:
```
x = np.asarray(x,float)
n=x.size
if target is None: target=n//2
# sort descending values and indices
order = np.argsort(x)[::-1]
h = np.zeros(n,int)
h[order[:target]] = 1
return h
```
This maximizes sum threshold. But if values ties, deterministic. For top-k.
`binary_threshold(x, t, rng)`:
```
h = (x >= t).astype(int)
s = h.sum()
target = n//2
if s > target: demote ones with lowest x to 0 until target.
elif s < target: promote zeros with highest x to1 until target.
```
Need tie random? Use stable sort.

Binary local optimize function:
```
def optimize_binary_local(h0, time_limit, max_passes=2000, rng=None, anneal=True):
   n=len(h0); target=n//2
   h = binary_from_float(h0,target)
   pos,neg = compute_pos_neg(h)
   M = int(max(pos.max(), neg.max()))
   best_M = M
   best_h = h.copy()
   best_c = (2*best_M)/n
   # But continuous C for binary = compute_c5 exactly = max(corr)*2/n. pos/neg raw counts, correlate raw. C = max(pos,neg)*2/n.
   no_improve_passes=0
   while time < limit and passes<...:
       # full steepest all swaps
       found, new_M, new_h = find_best_swap(h,pos,neg,M)
       if found and (new_M < M or (new_M == M and maybe accept secondary)):
           h=..., M=..., pos,neg=recompute if not returned
           ...
       else:
           # try anneal/random walk
           if anneal and time remains:
              do random swaps accepting M <= M + T; maybe if < best_M update
           else break
   return best_h,best_c
```
But `find_best_swap` as above can return new_h and new_M, and perhaps delta to update? We can recompute pos/neg after.

`find_best_swap(h, pos, neg, M_current)`:
- Build matrices only for zeros/ones. If no zeros or ones return None.
- Use int32 for delta/pos to reduce memory. pos/neg int64 maybe but values ≤n. Cast to int32 for operations.
- Pseudo:
```
L=n-1
h64 = h.astype(np.int64)
zeros = np.nonzero(h==0)[0]
ones = np.nonzero(h==1)[0]
z = len(zeros); o=len(ones)
if z==0 or o==0: return None
D = np.arange(1,n)
p = np.arange(n)[:,None]
next_valid = p < (n - D[None,:])
prev_valid = p >= D[None,:]
nxt = np.clip(p+D,0,n-1); prv=np.clip(p-D,0,n-1)
nxt_vals = np.where(next_valid, h64[nxt], 0)
prv_vals = np.where(prev_valid, h64[prv], 0)
# int32
add_pos = (next_valid*(1-nxt_vals) - prev_valid*prv_vals)
add_neg = (prev_valid*(1-prv_vals) - next_valid*nxt_vals)
rem_pos = (next_valid*nxt_vals - prev_valid*prv_vals)
rem_neg = (prev_valid*prv_vals - next_valid*nxt_vals)
Apos = add_pos[zeros].astype(np.int32)
Aneg = add_neg[zeros].astype(np.int32)
pos32=pos.astype(np.int32); neg32=neg.astype(np.int32)
best_score = None; best_p=None; best_q=None
rows = np.arange(z)
for q in ones:
    Bp=rem_pos[q]; Bn=rem_neg[q]
    baseP = Apos + Bp[None,:] + pos32[None,:]
    baseQ = Aneg + Bn[None,:] + neg32[None,:]
    C = np.maximum(baseP, baseQ)
    idx = np.abs(zeros - q) - 1
    cp = np.where(zeros < q, 1, -1).astype(np.int32)
    cq = -cp
    Pidx = baseP[rows, idx]
    Qidx = baseQ[rows, idx]
    C[rows, idx] = np.maximum(Pidx + cp, Qidx + cq)
    rowmax = C.max(axis=1)
    # candidates with rowmax < best_score
    # We can find min rowmax; if less than global (initialized M_current) select.
    min_val = rowmax.min()
    if min_val < best_score: ... maybe need index
```

`fix_sum_projection(v, target)`:
```
def project_box_sum(v, target):
    v = np.asarray(v,float)
    lo=0.0; hi=1.0
    # find lambda such that sum(clip(v+lambda,0,1))=target using bisection
    lo = -v.max() - 2 # maybe
    hi = 1 - v.min() + 2
    # f increasing in lambda
    for _ in range(80):
        mid=(lo+hi)/2
        s=np.clip(v+mid,0,1).sum()
        if s < target: lo=mid
        else: hi=mid
    h=np.clip(v+(lo+hi)/2,0,1)
    # correct small residual by adjusting non-boundaries
    diff = target - h.sum()
    if abs(diff)>1e-12:
       # distribute to entries not at 0/1 or largest room
       if diff>0:
          mask = h < 1-1e-12
       else: mask = h > 1e-12
       if mask.any():
          per=diff/np.count_nonzero(mask)
          h[mask]=np.clip(h[mask]+per,0,1)
          # maybe second pass
    return h
```
But target n/2 maybe 90. Bisection 80* n ops; okay. Could use sorting for exact but bisection fine. Need many calls? SLSQP objective gradient doesn't need projection internally if SLSQP handles equality. For gradient descent maybe.

SLSQP with constraints will keep equality within tolerance. Use `fix_sum` similar to previous (iterative) for final, because easier exact sum within floating? The target is integer; after SLSQP, h float sum may be close. We can use `enforce_sum_float(h,target)` that adjusts non-extreme values by small amounts; if sum diff >1e-9, can use waterfilling. We can use project_box_sum again with v=h to adjust to exact sum. Since bisection computes target to floating precision; sum may be within 1e-12. Good.

Soft max objective/grad:
```
def c5_smooth_obj(x, tau, n, dx):
    h = np.clip(x,0,1)
    c = np.correlate(h, 1.0-h, 'full')
    m = c.max()
    z = (c-m)/tau
    e = np.exp(z)
    s = e.sum()
    return float((m + tau*np.log(s)) * dx)

def c5_smooth_jac(x, tau, n, dx):
    h = np.clip(x,0,1)
    c = np.correlate(h, 1.0-h, 'full')
    m = c.max()
    e = np.exp((c-m)/tau)
    w = e/e.sum()
    # w length 2n-1
    u = w[:n][::-1]
    v = np.zeros(n); v[1:] = w[n:]
    a = h
    b = 1.0 - h
    grad_a = np.zeros(n); grad_b=np.zeros(n)
    for p in range(n):
       if u[p] != 0:
          if p == 0: grad_a += u[p]*b; grad_b += u[p]*a
          else:
             grad_a[:-p] += u[p]*b[p:]
             grad_b[p:] += u[p]*a[:-p]
       if v[p] != 0:
          grad_a[p:] += v[p]*b[:-p]
          grad_b[:-p] += v[p]*a[p:]
    return dx * (grad_a - grad_b)
```
But for p=0, the v[0]=0; for u0 we add full both; the loop above if p==0 does both full, no v. For p>0, as above. However I combined grad_b for u[p] incorrectly? Earlier grad_b u_p: grad_b[p:] += u[p]*a[:-p] yes. For p=0, grad_b += u0*a. Good.

But in grad_a for p>0, left positive u[p] b[p:] to grad_a[:-p]. right negative v[p] b[:-p] to grad_a[p:]. Correct. The loop uses if u[p] and v[p]; but for p>0, u term and v term separate. Need be careful for p=0: no `[:-0]` weird.

We can vectorize loop? Fine.

Annealing random moves: Need maintain pos/neg current exactly. If using `apply_swap` deltas from current h, must compute based on current h. We can compute on the fly. Let's implement a fast function `swap_delta_binary(h,p,q)` returning delta_pos,delta_neg, cross idx and cp.
Given p zero, q one.
```
L=n-1; D=np.arange(1,n)
pos = np.zeros(L,int); neg=zeros
# add p
nv = (p + D < n); pv=(p-D>=0)
pos[nv] += 1 - h[p+D[nv]]
pos[pv] -= h[p-D[pv]]
neg[pv] += 1 - h[p-D[pv]]
neg[nv] -= h[p+D[nv]]
# remove q
nv2=(q+D<n); pv2=(q-D>=0)
pos[nv2] += h[q+D[nv2]]
pos[pv2] -= h[q-D[pv2]]
neg[pv2] += h[q-D[pv2]]
neg[nv2] -= h[q+D[nv2]]
# cross
d=abs(p-q); idx=d-1
if p<q: pos[idx]+=1; neg[idx]-=1
else: pos[idx]-=1; neg[idx]+=1
return pos,neg
```
Need if both p and q neighbors for same D causing pos entries double? That's correct: add and remove deltas plus cross. If p and q same? no. If p-d and q-d? formulas independent.

Check for p zero q one in n=2 [0,1]: D=1. add p: nv true h1=1 => pos+=0, neg-=1. remove q: nv2 false, pv2 true h0=0 => pos-=0,neg+=0. cross idx0 p<q pos+1,neg-1. delta pos=1,neg=-2? Starting pos0,neg1; final pos1,neg0: delta pos1,neg-1. Wait neg delta we computed add -1, cross -1 = -2? Mist! Let's recalc neg formula for add p (0->1): earlier delta_neg = (1-prev) - next with prev invalid, next=1 => -1. For current h=[0,1], Q_1 old = h1(1-h0)=1. After p=1,q still1 (intermediate), Q_1=1*(1-1)=0 delta -1. Remove q 1->0 with p=1 (intermediate): current Q=0, final [1,0] Q=0*h0? Actually final [1,0], Q_1=0*(1-1)=0 delta0. Now recompute precompute matrices correctly:
```
next_val = np.where(next_valid, h[nx], 0)
prev_val = np.where(prev_valid, h[pv], 0)
next_pos = np.where(next_valid, 1 - h[nx], 0)
prev_pos = np.where(prev_valid, h[pv], 0)
def_pos = next_pos - prev_pos
next_neg = np.where(next_valid, h[nx], 0)
prev_neg = np.where(prev_valid, 1 - h[pv], 0)
def_neg = prev_neg - next_neg
add_pos=def_pos; add_neg=def_neg; rem_pos=-def_pos; rem_neg=-def_neg
```

Then in per q:
```
baseP = Apos + rem_pos[q][None,:] + pos[None,:]
baseQ = Aneg + rem_neg[q][None,:] + neg[None,:]
C = np.maximum(baseP, baseQ)
idx = abs(zeros - q)-1
C[rows, idx] += 1
rowmax = C.max(axis=1)
```
This is exact! Much faster (no signed corrections). Great.

For random swap apply:
```
delta_pos = add_pos_row + rem_pos_q; delta_pos[idx]+=1
delta_neg = add_neg_row + rem_neg_q; delta_neg[idx]+=1
new_pos = pos + delta_pos; new_neg = neg + delta_neg
```
When applying to h, h[p]=1,h[q]=0.

This local search is solid.

Now, objective max for binary: `M_current = max(pos.max(), neg.max())`. If we accept a swap if new_M < M_current. Since C=2M/n. If no strict, maybe equal moves not. But equal moves might be needed? Could allow if `new_M == M_current` and state not visited and a secondary "energy" decreases. Since cross always +1 at one lag; maybe equal moves could redistribute. We can compute secondary as sum of top 5 counts perhaps. But for simplicity, skip equal. Multistart/anneal handles.

But note: Any swap that keeps M same might be necessary to reach lower M? Hill climbing strictly decreasing M might get stuck in local minima. We can do annealing with accepting equal/worse. Implement a `local_search_binary` with two modes:
- Deterministic steepest until no strict improvement.
- Then perturbation: randomly apply a number of swaps (accept if new_M <= M_current or with probability for worse) to escape, then steepest.
But if we accept equal random, could cycle; but then steepest. We can restart from best state (lowest M seen) after perturbation, not current escalated.

Procedure per start:
```
best_h,c = initial
state = initial
for rounds up to restarts:
    state, M = state
    # steepest strict
    while time and found improvement:
       apply best improvement
       update best if M < best_M
    # if no further improvement, perturb state from best? Use best_h as anchor, perturb.
    if rounds < max:
       new_state = perturb(best_h, k)  # k random swaps, maintain balance
       M = max counts
       # maybe if new_state M < M+small
       continue
```
Use best anchor for perturb to avoid drift. Choose k increasing (1,2,3...). Perturb function: perform k random swaps (zero<->one) updates h; recompute pos/neg. Return.

def optimize_binary_local(h0, deadline, max_time_s, rng, max_rounds=10):
    n=len(h0); target=n//2
    h = binary_from_float(h0,target)
    pos,neg=compute_pos_neg(h)
    M=max(...)
    best_h=h.copy(); best_M=M
    c = 2*best_M/n
    rounds=0
    while time<deadline and rounds<max_rounds:
       improved=False
       # steepest loop
       while time<deadline:
          res=find_best_swap(h,pos,neg,M)
          if res is None: break
          h,pos,neg,M=res
          if M < best_M:
             best_M=M; best_h=h.copy(); c=2*best_M/n
          improved=True
       if not improved:
          if rounds >= max_rounds or time<...: break
          # perturb from best_h
          k = min(1+rounds, 8)
          h = perturb_state(best_h,k,rng)
          pos,neg=compute_pos_neg(h); M=max
          if M < best_M: update
          rounds +=1
    return best_h, 2*best_M/n
```

def swap_direct(h, pos, neg, p, q):
   D=np.arange(1,len(h))
   delta_pos = np.zeros(L,int); ...
   # add p (s=1): next_term/prev_term, etc
   # remove q (s=-1): -base
   delta = add + rem
   idx=abs(p-q)-1; delta_pos[idx]+=1; delta_neg[idx]+=1
   new_pos=pos+delta_pos; new_neg=neg+delta_neg
   if (new_pos<0).any() or (new_neg<0).any(): recompute? But should not.
   h[p]=1; h[q]=0
   return h, new_pos,new_neg
```

`binary_threshold(x, t, target, rng)`:
```
h = (x >= t).astype(np.int64)
s = h.sum()
order = np.argsort(x)
if s > target:
    ones = np.where(h == 1)[0]
    # demote ones with smallest x
    ones_sorted = ones[np.argsort(x[ones])]
    for i in range(s-target): h[ones_sorted[i]]=0
elif s < target:
    zeros = np.where(h == 0)[0]
    zeros_sorted = zeros[np.argsort(x[zeros])[::-1]]
    for i in range(target-s): h[zeros_sorted[i]]=1
return h
```

Let's outline final code more concretely.

Top-level functions:

```
import numpy as np
import time
import functools
from scipy.optimize import minimize

def compute_c5(h):
    h = np.asarray(h, dtype=float)
    if h.size == 0: return float('inf')
    dx = 2.0 / len(h)
    corr = np.correlate(h, 1.0 - h, mode='full')
    return float(np.max(corr) * dx)
```
This matches evaluator.

`full_corr_raw(h)` maybe for int.

`project_box_sum(v, target)`:
Bisection 60 iterations. Need target float. If v contains nan? Replace with 0.5.
```
def project_box_sum(v, target):
    v = np.asarray(v, dtype=float).copy()
    v = np.where(np.isfinite(v), v, 0.5)
    target = float(target)
    # If target 0 or n, clip.
    # Determine lo,hi such that sum(f(lo))<=target<=sum(f(hi)).
    lo = -v.max() - 1.0
    hi = 1.0 - v.min() + 1.0
    for _ in range(80):
       mid = 0.5*(lo+hi)
       s = np.clip(v+mid,0.0,1.0).sum()
       if s < target: lo=mid
       else: hi=mid
    h=np.clip(v+0.5*(lo+hi),0,1)
    # small correction
    diff = target - h.sum()
    if abs(diff) > 1e-12 and h.size>0:
       if diff > 0:
           mask = h < 1.0 - 1e-13
       else:
           mask = h > 1e-13
       if mask.any():
           cnt = mask.sum()
           adj = diff / cnt
           h_new = np.clip(h + adj,0,1)
           # if clip changed, distribute residual again? use a few loops
           for _ in range(5):
              h = h_new
              diff = target - h.sum()
              if abs(diff) < 1e-13: break
              if diff >0: mask = h < 1-1e-13
              else: mask = h > 1e-13
              if not mask.any(): break
              h = np.clip(h + diff/mask.sum(),0,1)
    return h
```
For binary target with v many 0/1, bisection may give exactly? Not important.

`binary_from_float(x, target)`:
If x int exactly target ones, preserve (top-k may reorder ties). To preserve int, if integer and sum==target, return copy.
```
def binary_from_float(x, target):
    x = np.asarray(x, dtype=float)
    n = x.size; target=int(target)
    # if array already integer with sum target
    xv = np.rint(x)
    if np.allclose(x, xv, atol=1e-12) and int(np.sum(xv)) == target:
       return xv.astype(np.int64)
    order = np.argsort(x)[::-1]
    h = np.zeros(n, dtype=np.int64)
    h[order[:target]] = 1
    return h
```
If x has nans, use nan_to_num.

`binary_threshold(x, t, target)`:
```
x = np.asarray(x,float); n=x.size; target=int(target)
h = (x >= t).astype(np.int64)
s=h.sum()
if s>target:
   ones=np.where(h==1)[0]
   # sort by x ascending, demote
   idx = ones[np.argsort(x[ones], kind='stable')[:s-target]]
   h[idx]=0
elif s<target:
   zeros=np.where(h==0)[0]
   idx= zeros[np.argsort(x[zeros], kind='stable')[::-1][:target-s]]
   h[idx]=1
return h
```

`run_based_init(n, rng)`: Maybe implement simple:
```
def run_based_init(n, rng):
    target=n//2
    h=np.zeros(n,dtype=int)
    # generate random runs lengths summing n, alternate starting side
    pos=0; use_one = rng.random()<0.5
    while pos<n:
       runs = rng.integers(1, max(2, min(20,n-pos)+1))
       if use_one:
          h[pos:pos+runs]=1
       pos+=runs; use_one=not use_one
    # adjust sum to target by flipping random positions
    s=h.sum()
    while s>target:
       idx=np.nonzero(h==1)[0]; i=rng.integers(len(idx)); h[idx[i]]=0; s-=1
    while s<target:
       idx=np.nonzero(h==0)[0]; i=rng.integers(len(idx)); h[idx[i]]=1; s+=1
    return h
```
This yields clustery starts.

`compute_pos_neg(h)`:
```
def compute_pos_neg(h):
    h = h.astype(np.int64, copy=False)
    b = 1 - h
    corr = np.correlate(h, b, mode='full')
    n=len(h)
    pos = corr[:n-1].astype(np.int64)
    neg = corr[n:].astype(np.int64)  # length n-1
    return pos, neg
```
Check `corr[n:]` length (2n-1)-n = n-1. Good. For n=1? not. If h binary int, corr int? If h int8, correlate int64? We'll cast int64.

`build_toggle(h)`:
Let's implement returning arrays for all r: `add_pos, add_neg, rem_pos, rem_neg` int32/numpy.
```
def build_toggle(h):
    n=len(h); L=n-1
    h64=h.astype(np.int64)
    p=np.arange(n)[:,None]
    D=np.arange(1,n)[None,:]
    nv = p < (n - D)
    pv = p >= D
    nx = np.clip(p + D, 0, n-1)
    px = np.clip(p - D, 0, n-1)
    next_val = np.where(nv, h64[nx], 0)
    prev_val = np.where(pv, h64[px], 0)
    next_term = np.where(nv, h64[nx] -? 1 - next_val, 0)
```
Need `np.where(nv, 1 - h64[nx], 0)`. But h64[nx] computed for invalid nx clipped but where ignores.
```
    next_pos = np.where(nv, 1 - next_val, 0)
    prev_pos = np.where(pv, prev_val, 0)
    base_p = next_pos - prev_pos
    prev_neg = np.where(pv, 1 - prev_val, 0)
    next_neg = np.where(nv, next_val, 0)
    base_n = prev_neg - next_neg
    add_pos = base_p.astype(np.int32)
    add_neg = base_n.astype(np.int32)
    rem_pos = (-base_p).astype(np.int32)
    rem_neg = (-base_n).astype(np.int32)
    return add_pos, add_neg, rem_pos, rem_neg
```
Check base_p for p toggled +1. For p with both next and prev valid, base can be in {-1,0,1}. int32.
For p as zero? add matrix used only zeros; for ones rem. Good.

`find_best_swap(h, pos, neg, M)`:
Need zeros, ones. If z/o 0 return None.
Call build_toggle. Select Apos = add_pos[zeros], Aneg. Use int32.
Set best_score = M (strict). store `best_pi = -1`, `best_q = -1`.
Create `rows = np.arange(z)` outside.
Loop for q in ones:
```
    bp = rem_pos[q]
    bn = rem_neg[q]
    baseP = Apos + bp  # broadcasts to z x L? Apos int32, bp int32 -> int32
    baseP = baseP + pos32  # pos32 shape L
    baseQ = Aneg + bn
    baseQ = baseQ + neg32
    C = np.maximum(baseP, baseQ)
    idx = np.abs(zeros - q) - 1
    C[rows, idx] += 1
    rowmax = C.max(axis=1)
    # find min rowmax, if < best_score
    minv = int(rowmax.min())
    if minv < best_score:
        best_score = minv
        best_pi = int(np.argmin(rowmax))
        best_q = int(q)
```
But baseP reallocation: `baseP = Apos + bp + pos32` creates new. Good. Could optimize by not adding pos32 to Apos each q? `baseP = Apos + (pos32 + bp)` create temp. Fine.
Potential issue: `C = np.maximum(baseP, baseQ)` creates C. Then `C[rows, idx] += 1`. This modifies C only, not base. Good.
After loop, if best_pi == -1 return None.
Compute delta:
```

```

`swap_direct(h, pos, neg, p, q)`:
To avoid build toggle each time, compute delta using D and h.
```
def swap_direct(h, pos, neg, p, q):
    n=len(h); L=n-1; D=np.arange(1,n)
    delta_pos=np.zeros(L,dtype=np.int64); delta_neg=np.zeros(L,dtype=np.int64)
    # add p
    nv = p + D < n; pv = p - D >= 0
    delta_pos[nv] += 1 - h[p + D[nv]]
    delta_pos[pv] -= h[p - D[pv]]
    delta_neg[pv] += 1 - h[p - D[pv]]
    delta_neg[nv] -= h[p + D[nv]]
    # remove q: s=-1
    nv = q + D < n; pv = q - D >= 0
    # delta = -base
    delta_pos[nv] -= (1 - h[q + D[nv]])
    delta_pos[pv] += h[q - D[pv]]
    delta_neg[pv] -= (1 - h[q - D[pv]])
    delta_neg[nv] += h[q + D[nv]]
    idx = abs(p-q)-1
    delta_pos[idx] += 1
    delta_neg[idx] += 1
    new_pos = pos + delta_pos; new_neg = neg + delta_neg
    h = h.copy(); h[p]=1; h[q]=0
    if (new_pos<0).any() or (new_neg<0).any(): recompute
    return h,new_pos,new_neg
```
Let's verify remove q signs with direct formula: base for +1: next_term - prev for pos. rem = -next_term + prev. So `delta_pos[nv] -= (1 - h_next)` and `delta_pos[pv] += h_prev`. Good. Neg rem = -(prev_zero - next_one) = -prev_zero + next_one. So `delta_neg[pv] -= (1 - h_prev)`, `delta_neg[nv] += h_next`. Good. For q remove [0,1] p? q=1: nv false, pv true h0=0: delta_pos +=0; delta_neg -= (1-0)=-1. Good.

`perturb_state(h, count, rng, pos=None, neg=None)`:
```
def perturb_state(h, count, rng, pos=None, neg=None):
    h = h.copy().astype(np.int64)
    if pos is None: pos,neg=compute_pos_neg(h)
    for _ in range(count):
       zeros=np.nonzero(h==0)[0]; ones=np.nonzero(h==1)[0]
       if zeros.size==0 or ones.size==0: break
       p=int(rng.integers(zeros.size)); q=int(rng.integers(ones.size))
       h,pos,neg = swap_direct(h,pos,neg,int(zeros[p]),int(ones[q]))
    return h,pos,neg
```
Can use current pos/neg updated. If many swaps, recompute at end? direct exact. Maybe after 50 swaps, recompute to guard. Add:
```
if count >0:
   p,neg=compute_pos_neg(h) # to reset? But this loses exact? It should match. Recompute end.
```
Recompute end for safety, cost small.

`optimize_binary_local(h0, deadline, time_per_slot, rng, max_rounds=8)`:
```
def optimize_binary_local(h0, deadline, time_per_slot=None, rng=None, max_rounds=8):
    if rng is None: rng=np.random.default_rng(0)
    n=len(h0); target=n//2
    h = binary_from_float(h0, target)
    pos,neg=compute_pos_neg(h)
    M=current_M(pos,neg)
    best_h=h.copy(); best_M=M
    rounds=0
    while time.time() < deadline and rounds < max_rounds:
       # time for steepest maybe time_per_slot? But if fast, continue.
       improved_any=False
       # strict local descent from current
       while time.time() < deadline:
           res = find_best_swap(h,pos,neg,M)
           if res is None: break
           h,pos,neg,M = res
           improved_any=True
           if M < best_M:
               best_M=M; best_h=h.copy()
       if not improved_any:
           if rounds >= max_rounds: break
           # optional if time_per_slot and elapsed? We don't track start. Just perturb if enough time.
           k = 1 + rounds
           h = best_h.copy()
           # perturb from best to escape
           h,pos,neg = perturb_state(h,k,rng)
           M = current_M(pos,neg)
           if M < best_M: update
           rounds +=1
    # final recompute best? optional
    return best_h, float(2.0*best_M / n)
```
But this may spend much of deadline in first start if steepest keeps improving? It stops when local min. If fast, moves to perturbs up to max_rounds. Good. However if steepest from a new start takes many passes and reaches near end, we may not perturb; okay. Need avoid one start eating all budget: In run we pass deadline as absolute for that start, not global. Use `start_deadline = min(global_deadline, t_start + time_per_start)`. Pass to optimize. It should respect. Good.

`time_per_start` dynamic. If local min found before deadline, it will do rounds and stop; could use remaining slot time for perturb rounds. If perturb then steepest fast, it may finish early and return. Good.

Potential issue: `find_best_swap` returns best strict improvement; if there is a zero-cost? No.

`c5_smooth_obj(x, tau, dx)`:
Need n from len(x). Use raw c. If tau<=0, return max(c)*dx. For safety tau = max(tau,1e-6).
```
def c5_smooth_obj(x, tau, dx):
    h = np.clip(x,0,1)
    c = np.correlate(h, 1.0-h, 'full')
    m = float(np.max(c))
    if tau <= 0: return m*dx
    e = np.exp((c - m) / tau)
    s = e.sum()
    if s == 0: return m*dx
    return float((m + tau * np.log(s)) * dx)
```
Could add tiny weight for all to avoid exp underflow? all <=1, sum>=1. fine.

`c5_smooth_jac(x, tau, dx)`:
```
def c5_smooth_jac(x, tau, dx):
    h = np.clip(x,0,1)
    n = len(h)
    c = np.correlate(h, 1.0-h, 'full')
    m = np.max(c)
    if tau <= 0:
        q = int(np.argmax(c))
        w = np.zeros(2*n-1); w[q]=1
    else:
        e = np.exp((c-m)/tau); s=e.sum(); w=e/s
    left = w[:n][::-1]
    v = np.zeros(n, dtype=float)
    if n > 1: v[1:] = w[n:]
    a=h; b=1.0-h
    grad_a=np.zeros(n); grad_b=np.zeros(n)
    for p in range(n):
        up = left[p]
        if up != 0.0:
            if p == 0:
                grad_a += up * b
                grad_b += up * a
            else:
                grad_a[:-p] += up * b[p:]
                grad_b[p:] += up * a[:-p]
        if p > 0:
            vv = v[p]
            if vv != 0.0:
                grad_a[p:] += vv * b[:-p]
                grad_b[:-p] += vv * a[p:]
    return dx * (grad_a - grad_b)
```
Need verify grad_b for p=0 includes u0 full; yes. For p>0 and u, our loop `grad_b[p:] += up*a[:-p]`. For p>0 v, `grad_b[:-p] += vv*a[p:]`. Good.
Optimization: if p large, slices empty? For p=n? p up to n-1; b[:-p] length n-p. ok.

Could combine for p=0 v=0. Good.

`sum_constraint(x, target)`:
```
def sum_constraint(x, target):
    return np.array([float(x.sum() - target)], dtype=float)
```
`sum_jacobian(x, target)`:
```
def sum_jacobian(x, target):
    return np.ones((1, x.size), dtype=float)
```

`run_slsqp_contin(h0, deadline, tau_schedule, maxiter_schedule, n_cap?)`:
```
def run_slsqp_contin(h0, deadline, tau_schedule, maxiter_schedule):
    h = np.asarray(h0, dtype=float)
    n=h.size; target=n/2.0; dx=2.0/n
    h = project_box_sum(h, target)
    best=h; bestc=compute_c5(h)
    bounds=[(0.0,1.0) for _ in range(n)]
    for tau, maxiter in zip(tau_schedule, maxiter_schedule):
        if time.time() >= deadline: break
        obj = functools.partial(c5_smooth_obj, tau=tau, dx=dx)
        jac = functools.partial(c5_smooth_jac, tau=tau, dx=dx)
        con = functools.partial(sum_constraint, target=target)
        jaccon = functools.partial(sum_jacobian, target=target)
        try:
            res = minimize(obj, h, jac=jac, method='SLSQP', bounds=bounds,
                           constraints=[{'type':'eq','fun':con,'jac':jaccon}],
                           options={'maxiter': maxiter, 'ftol': 1e-12, 'disp': False})
            h_new = np.clip(res.x,0,1)
            h_new = project_box_sum(h_new, target)
            c_new = compute_c5(h_new)
            if c_new < bestc - 1e-14:
                bestc = c_new; best = h_new.copy(); h = h_new
            else:
                # still use res.x as next starting point if finite?
                if np.all(np.isfinite(res.x)):
                    h = project_box_sum(np.clip(res.x,0,1), target)
        except Exception:
            pass
    return best, bestc
```
But if c_new not lower, using res.x may worsen; subsequent tau smaller might still find? Could keep best for final but continue from res.x? If res.x worse, maybe not. Use h = best for next tau to avoid drift? SLSQP local refinement from best likely better. If previous tau found a smoother point not lower in exact C but lower in soft, maybe continuing from it helps. But to be safe, if c_new < bestc set h=h_new; else h = project(res.x) maybe. Could get stuck. We can keep a `current` variable; final best separate. If current worsens, later tau may not recover. But tau reduction uses current; maybe soft optimum leads to exact. We can accept current even if exact not improved. At final return best. For next start, use current. Good.

Need `functools.partial` with keyword args? `functools.partial(c5_smooth_obj, tau=tau, dx=dx)`; minimize calls fun(x) only, okay. For constraints partial with target.

`upsample_to(h_old,n_new)`:
```
def upsample_to(h_old,n_new):
    n_old=len(h_old)
    if n_old == n_new: return h_old.astype(float).copy()
    x_old=np.linspace(0.0,2.0,n_old,endpoint=False)
    x_new=np.linspace(0.0,2.0,n_new,endpoint=False)
    return np.interp(x_new,x_old,h_old.astype(float))
```

Potential problem in `c5_smooth_jac`: The gradient of max over all lags includes weights summing 1. For tau small, w concentrated on active max. If multiple active, average. Good. But c vector length 2n-1; in mapping, left and right weights maybe not aligned with u/v? Let's verify mapping one more time with full c index. `np.correlate(h, 1-h, 'full')`: index 0 = h[0]*(1-h[1])? Earlier for n=3? Let's derive using convolution: c_full = correlate(a,b)=conv(a,b[::-1]). For n=3, b reversed [b2,b1,b0]; conv output indices 0..4:
0: a0*b2 = h0(1-h2) (P_2)
1: a0*b1 + a1*b2 = h0(1-h1)+h1(1-h2) (P_1)
2: a0*b0+a1*b1+a2*b2 = sum h_i(1-h_i) (lag0)
3: a1*b0+a2*b1 = h1(1-h0)+h2(1-h1) (Q_1)
4: a2*b0 = h2(1-h0) (Q_2)
Thus left tail length n: indices 0..2: P_2,P_1,P_0. u[p]=weight index n-1-p. p=0 -> index2 (lag0); p=1 -> index1; p=2 -> index0. So u= w[:n][::-1] yes.
Right tail indices3..4: Q_1,Q_2. v[1]=w[3],v[2]=w[4]. Good.
Grad formulas using u_p for offset +p in grad_a? For p=2, u2 weight P_2. grad_a term left positive h_i(1-h_{i+p}) with p=2: only i=0, grad_a[0]+=u2*b2; our loop p=2: `grad_a[:-2] += u2*b[2:]` -> grad_a[0]+=b2 correct. Right for p=2, v2 Q_2: term a_{i+p} b_i with i=0, a2*b0; grad_a[2:]? v2 term in grad_a: for a_k, p≤k? k=2: v2*b0; loop p=2: `grad_a[p:] += v2*b[:-p]` -> grad_a[2:]+=v2*b[0] correct. Good.

Let's draft code in analysis and check syntax.

```
import numpy as np
import time
import functools
from scipy.optimize import minimize

def compute_c5(h):
    h = np.asarray(h, dtype=float)
    if h.size == 0:
        return float('inf')
    n = h.size
    dx = 2.0 / n
    corr = np.correlate(h, 1.0 - h, mode='full')
    return float(np.max(corr) * dx)
```

`project_box_sum`:
Need target could be int. If target <0 or >n, adjust.
```
def project_box_sum(v, target):
    v = np.asarray(v, dtype=float).copy()
    if v.size ==0: return v
    target = float(min(max(target,0.0), float(v.size)))
    v = np.where(np.isfinite(v), v, 0.5)
    lo = -float(np.max(v)) - 1.0
    hi = 1.0 - float(np.min(v)) + 1.0
    for _ in range(70):
       mid = 0.5*(lo+hi)
       s = np.clip(v + mid, 0.0, 1.0).sum()
       if s < target: lo = mid
       else: hi = mid
    h = np.clip(v + 0.5*(lo+hi), 0.0, 1.0)
    # correction
    for _ in range(6):
       diff = target - float(h.sum())
       if abs(diff) < 1e-13: break
       if diff > 0:
           mask = h < 1.0 - 1e-13
       else:
           mask = h > 1e-13
       if not np.any(mask): break
       h[mask] = np.clip(h[mask] + diff / float(np.count_nonzero(mask)), 0.0, 1.0)
    return h
```
Bisection 70 might be overkill but fine.

`binary_from_float`:
```
def binary_from_float(x, target):
    x = np.asarray(x, dtype=float)
    x = np.where(np.isfinite(x), x, 0.0)
    n = x.size; target = int(target)
    if target <=0: return np.zeros(n, dtype=np.int64)
    if target >= n: return np.ones(n, dtype=np.int64)
    xv = np.rint(x)
    if np.allclose(x, xv, atol=1e-12, rtol=0.0) and int(np.sum(xv)) == target:
        return (xv >= 0.5).astype(np.int64) # xv maybe 0/1? if continuous? If allclose to integers but sum target, cast.
    order = np.argsort(x, kind='stable')[::-1]
    h = np.zeros(n, dtype=np.int64)
    h[order[:target]] = 1
    return h
```
If xv integer but includes negative? x in [0,1] hopefully. Use `(xv > 0.5)`.

`binary_threshold`:
```
def binary_threshold(x, t, target):
    x = np.asarray(x, dtype=float)
    x = np.where(np.isfinite(x), x, 0.0)
    n=x.size; target=int(target)
    h = (x >= t).astype(np.int64)
    s = int(h.sum())
    if s > target:
       ones = np.nonzero(h == 1)[0]
       if s - target > 0:
          idx = ones[np.argsort(x[ones], kind='stable')[:s-target]]
          h[idx]=0
    elif s < target:
       zeros = np.nonzero(h == 0)[0]
       if target - s > 0:
          idx = zeros[np.argsort(x[zeros], kind='stable')[::-1][:target-s]]
          h[idx]=1
    return h
```

`random_balanced_binary`:
```
def random_balanced_binary(n, rng):
    n=int(n); target=n//2
    h=np.zeros(n,dtype=np.int64)
    if target>0:
       idx = rng.permutation(n)[:target]
       h[idx]=1
    return h
```

`run_based_init`:
```
def run_based_init(n, rng):
    n=int(n); target=n//2
    h=np.zeros(n,dtype=np.int64)
    pos=0; use_one = bool(rng.random() < 0.5)
    min_run=1; max_run=min(20, n)
    while pos < n:
       max_r = max(min_run, min(max_run, n-pos))
       r = int(rng.integers(min_run, max_r + 1))
       if use_one:
          h[pos:pos+r] = 1
       pos += r
       use_one = not use_one
    s = int(h.sum())
    while s > target:
       ones = np.nonzero(h == 1)[0]
       if ones.size == 0: break
       i = int(rng.integers(ones.size))
       h[ones[i]] = 0; s -= 1
    while s < target:
       zeros = np.nonzero(h == 0)[0]
       if zeros.size == 0: break
       i = int(rng.integers(zeros.size))
       h[zeros[i]] = 1; s += 1
    return h
```
If random runs produce target off by maybe many; while okay.

`upsample_to`:
```
def upsample_to(h_old, n_new):
    h_old = np.asarray(h_old, dtype=float)
    n_old = h_old.size
    n_new = int(n_new)
    if n_old == n_new:
        return h_old.copy()
    if n_old == 0:
        return np.full(n_new, 0.5)
    x_old = np.linspace(0.0, 2.0, n_old, endpoint=False)
    x_new = np.linspace(0.0, 2.0, n_new, endpoint=False)
    return np.interp(x_new, x_old, h_old)
```

`compute_pos_neg` as above.

`current_max`:
```
def current_max(pos,neg):
    if pos.size == 0: return 0
    a = int(pos.max()) if pos.size else 0
    b = int(neg.max()) if neg.size else 0
    return max(a,b)
```

`build_toggle` as above. Need handle L=0? If n=1, D empty; `np.arange(1,n)` empty shape (0). p shape (1,1). `nv = p < (n - D)` works shape (1,0). Return empty arrays. Fine.

```
def build_toggle(h):
    n = int(h.size); L = n - 1
    h64 = h.astype(np.int64, copy=False)
    p = np.arange(n, dtype=np.int64)[:, None]
    D = np.arange(1, n, dtype=np.int64)[None, :]
    if L == 0:
        empty = np.empty((n,0), dtype=np.int32)
        return empty, empty.copy(), empty.copy(), empty.copy()
    nv = p < (n - D)
    pv = p >= D
    nx = np.clip(p + D, 0, n - 1)
    px = np.clip(p - D, 0, n - 1)
    next_val = np.where(nv, h64[nx], 0)
    prev_val = np.where(pv, h64[px], 0)
    next_pos = np.where(nv, 1 - next_val, 0)
    prev_pos = np.where(pv, prev_val, 0)
    next_neg = np.where(nv, next_val, 0)
    prev_neg = np.where(pv, 1 - prev_val, 0)
    base_p = next_pos - prev_pos
    base_n = prev_neg - next_neg
    add_p = base_p.astype(np.int32, copy=False)
    add_n = base_n.astype(np.int32, copy=False)
    rem_p = (-base_p).astype(np.int32, copy=False)
    rem_n = (-base_n).astype(np.int32, copy=False)
    return add_p, add_n, rem_p, rem_n
```
But `empty.copy()` returns views? Need four distinct? Not important. But empty int32. In n>1, `base_p` int64, astype int32.

`find_best_swap` code:
```
def find_best_swap(h, pos, neg, M):
    n = int(h.size); target = n//2
    zeros = np.nonzero(h == 0)[0]
    ones = np.nonzero(h == 1)[0]
    if zeros.size == 0 or ones.size == 0:
        return None
    add_p, add_n, rem_p, rem_n = build_toggle(h)
    A_p = add_p[zeros]
    A_n = add_n[zeros]
    pos32 = pos.astype(np.int32, copy=False)
    neg32 = neg.astype(np.int32, copy=False)
    rows = np.arange(zeros.size, dtype=np.intp)
    best_score = int(M)
    best_pi = -1
    best_q = -1
    for q in ones:
        bp = rem_p[q]
        bn = rem_n[q]
        baseP = A_p + bp + pos32
        baseQ = A_n + bn + neg32
        C = np.maximum(baseP, baseQ)
        idx = np.abs(zeros - q) - 1
        C[rows, idx] += 1
        rowmax = C.max(axis=1)
        minv = int(rowmax.min())
        if minv < best_score:
            best_score = minv
            best_pi = int(np.argmin(rowmax))
            best_q = int(q)
    if best_pi < 0:
        return None
    p = int(zeros[best_pi]); q = int(best_q)
    delta_p = A_p[best_pi].astype(np.int64, copy=False) + rem_p[q].astype(np.int64, copy=False)
    delta_n = A_n[best_pi].astype(np.int64, copy=False) + rem_n[q].astype(np.int64, copy=False)
    idx = int(abs(p - q) - 1)
    delta_p[idx] += 1
    delta_n[idx] += 1
    new_pos = pos.astype(np.int64, copy=False) + delta_p
    new_neg = neg.astype(np.int64, copy=False) + delta_n
    new_h = h.copy()
    new_h[p] = 1
    new_h[q] = 0
    if (new_pos < 0).any() or (new_neg < 0).any():
        new_pos, new_neg = compute_pos_neg(new_h)
    new_M = current_max(new_pos, new_neg)
    if new_M != best_score:
        # recompute to be safe
        new_pos, new_neg = compute_pos_neg(new_h)
        new_M = current_max(new_pos, new_neg)
    if new_M >= int(M):
        # maybe due safety; recompute and if no improvement return None? But best_score < M; if recompute says >=M, bug. Return None to be safe.
        return None
    return new_h, new_pos, new_neg, new_M
```
Important: `A_p[best_pi].astype` if `A_p` is non-contiguous; okay. `compute_posneg` expects int h; new_h int. pos/neg arrays int64. Good.

One issue: `baseP = A_p + bp + pos32` where `bp` int32 shape L, `pos32` int32. If `A_p` is int32. But `A_p + bp` first returns int32. Fine. However if `A_p` is empty? zeros size >0.

Memory: C is result of `np.maximum`, maybe int32. `C[rows, idx] +=1` modifies. When idx array dtype int64, okay.

Potential micro-optimization: `rows` created each call. Fine.

`swap_direct` code:
```
def swap_direct(h, pos, neg, p, q):
    n = int(h.size); L = n - 1
    h64 = h.astype(np.int64, copy=False)
    delta_p = np.zeros(L, dtype=np.int64)
    delta_n = np.zeros(L, dtype=np.int64)
    if L > 0:
       D = np.arange(1, n, dtype=np.int64)
       # p add
       nv = p + D < n
       pv = p - D >= 0
       if np.any(nv):
          delta_p[nv] += 1 - h64[p + D[nv]]
          delta_n[nv] -= h64[p + D[nv]]
       if np.any(pv):
          delta_p[pv] -= h64[p - D[pv]]
          delta_n[pv] += 1 - h64[p - D[pv]]
       # q remove
       nv2 = q + D < n
       pv2 = q - D >= 0
       if np.any(nv2):
          delta_p[nv2] -= (1 - h64[q + D[nv2]])
          delta_n[nv2] += h64[q + D[nv2]]
       if np.any(pv2):
          delta_p[pv2] += h64[q - D[pv2]]
          delta_n[pv2] -= (1 - h64[q - D[pv2]])
       idx = int(abs(p - q) - 1)
       delta_p[idx] += 1
       delta_n[idx] += 1
    new_pos = pos + delta_p
    new_neg = neg + delta_n
    new_h = h.copy()
    new_h[p] = 1
    new_h[q] = 0
    if (new_pos < 0).any() or (new_neg < 0).any():
       new_pos, new_neg = compute_pos_neg(new_h)
    return new_h, new_pos, new_neg
```
Check sign for p add neg: delta_neg = s*((1-prev)-next). For p add: prev_term to delta_neg += 1 - h_prev; next_term subtract h_next. We do delta_n[pv] += 1 - h_prev; delta_n[nv] -= h_next. Good.
For q remove: s=-1: delta_neg = -((1-prev)-next) = -1+prev+next. We do pv: -= (1 - prev); nv: += next. Good. Pos remove: pv += prev; nv -= (1-next). Good.

`perturb_state`:
```
def perturb_state(h, count, rng, pos=None, neg=None):
    h = h.copy().astype(np.int64, copy=False)
    if pos is None or neg is None:
        pos,neg = compute_pos_neg(h)
    count = int(count)
    for k in range(count):
       zeros = np.nonzero(h == 0)[0]
       ones = np.nonzero(h == 1)[0]
       if zeros.size == 0 or ones.size == 0: break
       pi = int(rng.integers(zeros.size)); qi = int(rng.integers(ones.size))
       h,pos,neg = swap_direct(h,pos,neg,int(zeros[pi]),int(ones[qi]))
    # recompute final to guard
    pos,neg = compute_pos_neg(h)
    return h,pos,neg
```
This recomputes end cost. Good.

`optimize_binary_local`:
Need ensure it doesn't modify h0. It copies. Use `deadline` absolute.
```
def optimize_binary_local(h0, deadline, rng, max_rounds=8):
    h = binary_from_float(h0, len(h0)//2)
    pos, neg = compute_pos_neg(h)
    M = current_max(pos, neg)
    best_h = h.copy()
    best_M = M
    rounds = 0
    while time.time() < deadline and rounds < max_rounds:
        improved = False
        while time.time() < deadline:
            res = find_best_swap(h, pos, neg, M)
            if res is None:
                break
            h, pos, neg, M = res
            improved = True
            if M < best_M:
                best_M = M
                best_h = h.copy()
        if not improved:
            if rounds >= max_rounds:
                break
            k = min(1 << rounds, 16)
            # perturb from best_h
            h, pos, neg = perturb_state(best_h, k, rng)
            M = current_max(pos, neg)
            if M < best_M:
                best_M = M
                best_h = h.copy()
            rounds += 1
    # final if current M better?
    if M < best_M:
        best_M = M
        best_h = h.copy()
    return best_h, float(2.0 * best_M / max(1, len(best_h)))
```
Potential bug: In perturb round, if h current local min different from best_h? We perturb from best_h each round; but current h after steepest should be best_h if only strict improvements. If equal not accepted, yes. Good. k increases. If first round no improvement, k=1; next k=2; etc.
If slots very short, may do only one.

But if `find_best_swap` repeatedly finds improvements and never reaches no improvement before deadline, it won't perturb; returns best. Good.

`add_state` top-level:
```
def add_state(states, c, h, n, limit=4):
    # states list of [c, h, n]
    if h is None or not np.isfinite(c): return
    h_arr = np.asarray(h, dtype=np.int64)
    if len(states) < limit:
        states.append([c, h_arr.copy(), int(n)])
        return
    max_i = 0
    max_c = -1.0
    for i, st in enumerate(states):
        if st[0] > max_c:
            max_c = st[0]; max_i = i
    if c < max_c - 1e-15:
        states[max_i] = [c, h_arr.copy(), int(n)]
```
We can use states for check maybe not.

`run_slsqp_contin` need `deadline` absolute. We can also pass `max_total_s`. Use `t0`? Just check time.time.
```
def run_slsqp_contin(h0, deadline):
    h0 = np.asarray(h0, dtype=float)
    n = h0.size
    if n == 0 or time.time() >= deadline: return h0, compute_c5(h0)
    target = n / 2.0
    dx = 2.0 / n
    h = project_box_sum(h0, target)
    best_h = h.copy()
    best_c = compute_c5(h)
    bounds = [(0.0, 1.0) for _ in range(n)]
    if n <= 180:
        taus = [0.15,0.08,0.04,0.02,0.01]
        iters = [150,200,250,300,400]
    elif n <= 360:
        taus = [0.15,0.08,0.04,0.02]
        iters = [150,200,250,300]
    else:
        taus = [0.15,0.08,0.04]
        iters = [100,150,200]
    current = h
    for tau, maxiter in zip(taus, iters):
        if time.time() + 5.0 >= deadline:
            break
        try:
            obj_p = functools.partial(c5_smooth_obj, tau=tau, dx=dx)
            jac_p = functools.partial(c5_smooth_jac, tau=tau, dx=dx)
            con_p = functools.partial(sum_constraint, target=target)
            jaccon_p = functools.partial(sum_jacobian, target=target)
            res = minimize(obj_p, current, jac=jac_p, method='SLSQP',
                           bounds=bounds,
                           constraints=[{'type':'eq','fun':con_p,'jac':jaccon_p}],
                           options={'maxiter': maxiter, 'ftol': 1e-12, 'disp': False})
            if res.x is not None and np.all(np.isfinite(res.x)):
                trial = np.clip(res.x, 0.0, 1.0)
                trial = project_box_sum(trial, target)
                c_trial = compute_c5(trial)
                if c_trial < best_c - 1e-14:
                    best_c = c_trial
                    best_h = trial.copy()
                    current = trial.copy()
                else:
                    current = trial.copy()
        except Exception:
            pass
    # final maybe try rounding
    h_round = binary_from_float(best_h, n//2).astype(float)
    c_round = compute_c5(h_round)
    if c_round < best_c:
       best_c = c_round; best_h = h_round
    return best_h, best_c
```
But if current worsens, next tau may not recover; but best saved. Could instead if c_trial >= best_c, keep current = best_h? Maybe better. Since if current worse, soft optimum might still lead to better with smaller tau? Hard. To avoid losing, we can set current = trial only if c_trial < best_h exact? But then no follow-up from smoother. Track both `current_exact_best` and `current_soft`. We can do: if c_trial < best_c: best update, current=trial. Else if c_trial < current_base_c? maybe. Simpler: current = trial; even if worse, smaller tau may refine. At final best remains.

Could SLSQP from binary with tau small return same due nonconvex; okay.

```
def exact_sum_adjust(h, target):
   h = h.copy()
   diff = target - h.sum()
   if diff ==0: return h
   # If h binary int, sum exact. For float, choose index with room and add diff if within [0,1]
   if diff >0:
      mask = h < 1.0 - abs(diff) - 1e-15
   else:
      mask = h > abs(diff) + 1e-15
   if mask.any():
      i=np.argmax(mask)
      h[i] += diff
      return np.clip(h,0,1)
   return project_box_sum(h,target)
```
But adding arbitrary diff may make one value not exact sum due floating, but close. Project enough.

Maybe for continuous final we can force exact sum by making one coordinate take residual if within bounds. This can yield sum exactly in floating? `h[i] += diff` where diff = target - h.sum(); due floating, new sum may be target plus rounding but likely closer. If diff large, no. We can call project.

def finalize_h(h):
   h = np.asarray(h, dtype=float)
   n=h.size; target=n/2.0
   h = project_box_sum(h,target)

         r = (r > 0.5).astype(float)
         if r.sum() != n/2:
            r = binary_from_float(h, n//2).astype(float)
      return r
   return h

```python
import numpy as np
import time
import functools
from scipy.optimize import minimize


def compute_c5(h):
    h = np.asarray(h, dtype=float)
    if h.size == 0:
        return float("inf")
    n = h.size
    dx = 2.0 / n
    corr = np.correlate(h, 1.0 - h, mode="full")
    return float(np.max(corr) * dx)


def project_box_sum(v, target):
    v = np.asarray(v, dtype=float).copy()
    if v.size == 0:
        return v
    target = float(min(max(target, 0.0), float(v.size)))
    v = np.where(np.isfinite(v), v, 0.5)

    xv = np.rint(v)
    if np.allclose(v, xv, atol=1e-12, rtol=0.0) and abs(float(xv.sum()) - target) <= 1e-9:
        return np.clip(xv, 0.0, 1.0)

    lo = -float(np.max(v)) - 1.0
    hi = 1.0 - float(np.min(v)) + 1.0
    for _ in range(70):
        mid = 0.5 * (lo + hi)
        s = float(np.clip(v + mid, 0.0, 1.0).sum())
        if s < target:
            lo = mid
        else:
            hi = mid

    h = np.clip(v + 0.5 * (lo + hi), 0.0, 1.0)
    for _ in range(8):
        diff = target - float(h.sum())
        if abs(diff) < 1e-13:
            break
        if diff > 0:
            mask = h < 1.0 - 1e-13
        else:
            mask = h > 1e-13
        if not np.any(mask):
            break
        h[mask] = np.clip(h[mask] + diff / float(np.count_nonzero(mask)), 0.0, 1.0)
    return h


def binary_from_float(x, target):
    x = np.asarray(x, dtype=float)
    x = np.where(np.isfinite(x), x, 0.0)
    n = x.size
    target = int(target)
    if target <= 0:
        return np.zeros(n, dtype=np.int64)
    if target >= n:
        return np.ones(n, dtype=np.int64)

    xv = np.rint(x)
    if np.allclose(x, xv, atol=1e-12, rtol=0.0) and int(np.sum(xv)) == target:
        return (xv > 0.5).astype(np.int64)

    order = np.argsort(x, kind="stable")[::-1]
    h = np.zeros(n, dtype=np.int64)
    h[order[:target]] = 1
    return h


def binary_threshold(x, t, target):
    x = np.asarray(x, dtype=float)
    x = np.where(np.isfinite(x), x, 0.0)
    n = x.size
    target = int(target)
    h = (x >= float(t)).astype(np.int64)
    s = int(h.sum())
    if s > target:
        ones = np.nonzero(h == 1)[0]
        if ones.size:
            idx = ones[np.argsort(x[ones], kind="stable")[: s - target]]
            h[idx] = 0
    elif s < target:
        zeros = np.nonzero(h == 0)[0]
        if zeros.size:
            idx = zeros[np.argsort(x[zeros], kind="stable")[::-1][: target - s]]
            h[idx] = 1
    return h


def random_balanced_binary(n, rng):
    n = int(n)
    target = n // 2
    h = np.zeros(n, dtype=np.int64)
    if target > 0:
        idx = rng.permutation(n)[:target]
        h[idx] = 1
    return h


def run_based_init(n, rng):
    n = int(n)
    target = n // 2
    h = np.zeros(n, dtype=np.int64)
    pos = 0
    use_one = bool(rng.random() < 0.5)
    max_run = min(20, n)
    while pos < n:
        max_r = max(1, min(max_run, n - pos))
        r = int(rng.integers(1, max_r + 1))
        if use_one:
            h[pos:pos + r] = 1
        pos += r
        use_one = not use_one

    s = int(h.sum())
    while s > target:
        ones = np.nonzero(h == 1)[0]
        if ones.size == 0:
            break
        i = int(rng.integers(ones.size))
        h[ones[i]] = 0
        s -= 1
    while s < target:
        zeros = np.nonzero(h == 0)[0]
        if zeros.size == 0:
            break
        i = int(rng.integers(zeros.size))
        h[zeros[i]] = 1
        s += 1
    return h


def upsample_to(h_old, n_new):
    h_old = np.asarray(h_old, dtype=float)
    n_old = h_old.size
    n_new = int(n_new)
    if n_old == n_new:
        return h_old.copy()
    if n_old == 0:
        return np.full(n_new, 0.5, dtype=float)
    x_old = np.linspace(0.0, 2.0, n_old, endpoint=False)
    x_new = np.linspace(0.0, 2.0, n_new, endpoint=False)
    return np.interp(x_new, x_old, h_old)


def compute_pos_neg(h):
    h = h.astype(np.int64, copy=False)
    b = 1 - h
    corr = np.correlate(h, b, mode="full")
    n = h.size
    pos = corr[:n - 1].astype(np.int64, copy=False)
    neg = corr[n:].astype(np.int64, copy=False)
    return pos, neg


def current_max(pos, neg):
    if pos.size == 0:
        return 0
    a = int(pos.max())
    b = int(neg.max())
    return max(a, b)


def build_toggle(h):
    n = int(h.size)
    L = n - 1
    h64 = h.astype(np.int64, copy=False)
    if L == 0:
        empty = np.empty((n, 0), dtype=np.int32)
        return empty, empty.copy(), empty.copy(), empty.copy()

    p = np.arange(n, dtype=np.int64)[:, None]
    D = np.arange(1, n, dtype=np.int64)[None, :]

    nv = p < (n - D)
    pv = p >= D
    nx = np.clip(p + D, 0, n - 1)
    px = np.clip(p - D, 0, n - 1)

    next_val = np.where(nv, h64[nx], 0)
    prev_val = np.where(pv, h64[px], 0)

    next_pos = np.where(nv, 1 - next_val, 0)
    prev_pos = np.where(pv, prev_val, 0)
    base_p = next_pos - prev_pos

    next_neg = np.where(nv, next_val, 0)
    prev_neg = np.where(pv, 1 - prev_val, 0)
    base_n = prev_neg - next_neg

    add_p = base_p.astype(np.int32, copy=False)
    add_n = base_n.astype(np.int32, copy=False)
    rem_p = (-base_p).astype(np.int32, copy=False)
    rem_n = (-base_n).astype(np.int32, copy=False)
    return add_p, add_n, rem_p, rem_n


def find_best_swap(h, pos, neg, M):
    h = h.astype(np.int64, copy=False)
    zeros = np.nonzero(h == 0)[0]
    ones = np.nonzero(h == 1)[0]
    if zeros.size == 0 or ones.size == 0:
        return None

    add_p, add_n, rem_p, rem_n = build_toggle(h)
    A_p = add_p[zeros]
    A_n = add_n[zeros]
    pos32 = pos.astype(np.int32, copy=False)
    neg32 = neg.astype(np.int32, copy=False)

    rows = np.arange(zeros.size, dtype=np.intp)
    best_score = int(M)
    best_pi = -1
    best_q = -1

    for q in ones:
        bp = rem_p[q]
        bn = rem_n[q]
        baseP = A_p + bp + pos32
        baseQ = A_n + bn + neg32
        C = np.maximum(baseP, baseQ)
        idx = np.abs(zeros - q) - 1
        C[rows, idx] += 1
        rowmax = C.max(axis=1)
        bi = int(np.argmin(rowmax))
        mv = int(rowmax[bi])
        if mv < best_score:
            best_score = mv
            best_pi = bi
            best_q = int(q)

    if best_pi < 0:
        return None

    p = int(zeros[best_pi])
    q = int(best_q)

    delta_p = A_p[best_pi].astype(np.int64, copy=False) + rem_p[q].astype(np.int64, copy=False)
    delta_n = A_n[best_pi].astype(np.int64, copy=False) + rem_n[q].astype(np.int64, copy=False)
    idx = int(abs(p - q) - 1)
    delta_p[idx] += 1
    delta_n[idx] += 1

    new_pos = pos.astype(np.int64, copy=False) + delta_p
    new_neg = neg.astype(np.int64, copy=False) + delta_n
    new_h = h.copy()
    new_h[p] = 1
    new_h[q] = 0

    if (new_pos < 0).any() or (new_neg < 0).any():
        new_pos, new_neg = compute_pos_neg(new_h)

    new_M = current_max(new_pos, new_neg)
    if new_M != best_score:
        new_pos, new_neg = compute_pos_neg(new_h)
        new_M = current_max(new_pos, new_neg)
    if new_M >= int(M):
        return None

    return new_h, new_pos, new_neg, new_M


def swap_direct(h, pos, neg, p, q):
    h = h.astype(np.int64, copy=False)
    n = int(h.size)
    L = n - 1
    delta_p = np.zeros(L, dtype=np.int64)
    delta_n = np.zeros(L, dtype=np.int64)

    if L > 0:
        D = np.arange(1, n, dtype=np.int64)

        nv = p + D < n
        pv = p - D >= 0
        if np.any(nv):
            delta_p[nv] += 1 - h[p + D[nv]]
            delta_n[nv] -= h[p + D[nv]]
        if np.any(pv):
            delta_p[pv] -= h[p - D[pv]]
            delta_n[pv] += 1 - h[p - D[pv]]

        nv2 = q + D < n
        pv2 = q - D >= 0
        if np.any(nv2):
            delta_p[nv2] -= (1 - h[q + D[nv2]])
            delta_n[nv2] += h[q + D[nv2]]
        if np.any(pv2):
            delta_p[pv2] += h[q - D[pv2]]
            delta_n[pv2] -= (1 - h[q - D[pv2]])

        idx = int(abs(p - q) - 1)
        delta_p[idx] += 1
        delta_n[idx] += 1

    new_pos = pos + delta_p
    new_neg = neg + delta_n
    new_h = h.copy()
    new_h[p] = 1
    new_h[q] = 0

    if (new_pos < 0).any() or (new_neg < 0).any():
        new_pos, new_neg = compute_pos_neg(new_h)

    return new_h, new_pos, new_neg


def perturb_state(h, count, rng, pos=None, neg=None):
    h = h.copy().astype(np.int64, copy=False)
    if pos is None or neg is None:
        pos, neg = compute_pos_neg(h)
    count = int(count)
    for _ in range(count):
        zeros = np.nonzero(h == 0)[0]
        ones = np.nonzero(h == 1)[0]
        if zeros.size == 0 or ones.size == 0:
            break
        pi = int(rng.integers(zeros.size))
        qi = int(rng.integers(ones.size))
        h, pos, neg = swap_direct(h, pos, neg, int(zeros[pi]), int(ones[qi]))

    pos, neg = compute_pos_neg(h)
    return h, pos, neg


def optimize_binary_local(h0, deadline, rng, max_rounds=10):
    n = int(len(h0))
    target = n // 2
    h = binary_from_float(h0, target)
    pos, neg = compute_pos_neg(h)
    M = current_max(pos, neg)

    best_h = h.copy()
    best_M = M
    rounds = 0
    base_k = max(2, int(np.ceil(n / 20.0)))

    while time.time() < deadline and rounds < max_rounds:
        improved = False
        while time.time() < deadline:
            res = find_best_swap(h, pos, neg, M)
            if res is None:
                break
            h, pos, neg, M = res
            improved = True
            if M < best_M:
                best_M = M
                best_h = h.copy()

        if not improved:
            if rounds >= max_rounds:
                break
            k = min(base_k + rounds, 64)
            h, pos, neg = perturb_state(best_h, k, rng)
            M = current_max(pos, neg)
            if M < best_M:
                best_M = M
                best_h = h.copy()
            rounds += 1

    if M < best_M:
        best_M = M
        best_h = h.copy()

    return best_h, float(2.0 * best_M / max(1, n))


def add_state(states, c, h, n, limit=4):
    if h is None or not np.isfinite(c):
        return
    h_arr = np.asarray(h, dtype=np.int64)
    if len(states) < limit:
        states.append([float(c), h_arr.copy(), int(n)])
        return
    max_i = 0
    max_c = -1.0
    for i, st in enumerate(states):
        if st[0] > max_c:
            max_c = st[0]
            max_i = i
    if float(c) < max_c - 1e-15:
        states[max_i] = [float(c), h_arr.copy(), int(n)]


def initial_parent():
    p = globals().get("initial_h_values", None)
    if p is not None:
        p = np.asarray(p, dtype=float).ravel()
        if p.size > 0 and np.all(np.isfinite(p)):
            return p
    return np.full(180, 0.5, dtype=float)


def c5_smooth_obj(x, tau, dx):
    h = np.clip(x, 0.0, 1.0)
    c = np.correlate(h, 1.0 - h, mode="full")
    m = float(np.max(c))
    tau = max(float(tau), 1e-6)
    e = np.exp((c - m) / tau)
    s = float(e.sum())
    if s <= 0.0:
        return m * float(dx)
    return float((m + tau * np.log(s)) * dx)


def c5_smooth_jac(x, tau, dx):
    h = np.clip(x, 0.0, 1.0)
    n = h.size
    c = np.correlate(h, 1.0 - h, mode="full")
    m = float(np.max(c))
    tau = max(float(tau), 1e-6)
    e = np.exp((c - m) / tau)
    s = float(e.sum())
    if s <= 0.0:
        w = np.zeros(2 * n - 1, dtype=float)
        w[int(np.argmax(c))] = 1.0
    else:
        w = e / s

    left = w[:n][::-1]
    v = np.zeros(n, dtype=float)
    if n > 1:
        v[1:] = w[n:]

    a = h
    b = 1.0 - h
    grad_a = np.zeros(n, dtype=float)
    grad_b = np.zeros(n, dtype=float)

    for p in range(n):
        up = float(left[p])
        if up != 0.0:
            if p == 0:
                grad_a += up * b
                grad_b += up * a
            else:
                grad_a[:-p] += up * b[p:]
                grad_b[p:] += up * a[:-p]

        if p > 0:
            vv = float(v[p])
            if vv != 0.0:
                grad_a[p:] += vv * b[:-p]
                grad_b[:-p] += vv * a[p:]

    return float(dx) * (grad_a - grad_b)


def sum_constraint(x, target):
    return np.array([float(np.sum(x) - target)], dtype=float)


def sum_jacobian(x, target):
    return np.ones((1, x.size), dtype=float)


def run_slsqp_contin(h0, deadline):
    h0 = np.asarray(h0, dtype=float)
    n = int(h0.size)
    if n == 0 or time.time() >= deadline:
        return h0, float("inf")

    target = n / 2.0
    dx = 2.0 / n

    h = project_box_sum(h0, target)
    best_h = h.copy()
    best_c = compute_c5(h)

    bounds = [(0.0, 1.0) for _ in range(n)]
    if n <= 180:
        taus = [0.15, 0.08, 0.04, 0.02, 0.01]
        iters = [160, 220, 280, 360, 420]
    elif n <= 360:
        taus = [0.15, 0.08, 0.04, 0.02]
        iters = [160, 220, 280, 340]
    else:
        taus = [0.15, 0.08, 0.04]
        iters = [120, 160, 200]

    current = h.copy()
    for tau, maxiter in zip(taus, iters):
        if time.time() + 4.0 >= deadline:
            break
        try:
            obj_p = functools.partial(c5_smooth_obj, tau=tau, dx=dx)
            jac_p = functools.partial(c5_smooth_jac, tau=tau, dx=dx)
            con_p = functools.partial(sum_constraint, target=target)
            jacc_p = functools.partial(sum_jacobian, target=target)

            res = minimize(
                obj_p,
                current,
                jac=jac_p,
                method="SLSQP",
                bounds=bounds,
                constraints=[{"type": "eq", "fun": con_p, "jac": jacc_p}],
                options={"maxiter": int(maxiter), "ftol": 1e-12, "disp": False},
            )

            if res.x is not None and np.all(np.isfinite(res.x)):
                trial = np.clip(res.x, 0.0, 1.0)
                trial = project_box_sum(trial, target)
                c_trial = compute_c5(trial)

                if np.isfinite(c_trial):
                    current = trial.copy()
                    if c_trial < best_c - 1e-14:
                        best_c = c_trial
                        best_h = trial.copy()
        except Exception:
            pass

    if n % 2 == 0:
        h_round = binary_from_float(best_h, n // 2).astype(float)
        c_round = compute_c5(h_round)
        if np.isfinite(c_round) and c_round < best_c:
            best_c = c_round
            best_h = h_round

    return best_h, best_c


def run(seed=42, budget_s=1300, **kwargs):
    t0 = time.time()
    budget_s = float(budget_s)
    deadline = t0 + budget_s
    rng = np.random.default_rng(int(seed))

    parent = initial_parent()
    parent_n = int(parent.size)
    parent = project_box_sum(parent, parent_n / 2.0)

    best_h = parent.copy()
    best_c = compute_c5(best_h)
    best_n = parent_n
    if not np.isfinite(best_c):
        best_h = random_balanced_binary(180, rng).astype(float)
        best_c = compute_c5(best_h)
        best_n = 180
        parent = best_h.copy()
        parent_n = 180

    reserve = max(30.0, 0.15 * budget_s)
    binary_deadline = min(deadline - reserve, deadline)
    if binary_deadline < t0 + 5.0:
        binary_deadline = deadline - 2.0

    res_set = set()
    for base in [parent_n, 180, 360, 720]:
        b = int(base)
        if b % 2:
            b += 1
        if 30 <= b <= 720:
            res_set.add(b)
    for m in (2, 4):
        b = int(parent_n) * m
        if b % 2:
            b += 1
        if 30 <= b <= 720:
            res_set.add(b)
    res_list = sorted(res_set)

    best_binary_states = []

    for n in res_list:
        if time.time() > binary_deadline - 25.0:
            break
        n = int(n)
        if n % 2 or n < 20:
            continue

        up = upsample_to(parent, n)

        if n <= 180:
            sigmas = (0.03, 0.06, 0.10, 0.18, 0.30)
            threshs = (0.20, 0.30, 0.40, 0.50, 0.60, 0.70, 0.80)
            rand_count = 6
            run_count = 4
            max_starts = 25
        elif n <= 360:
            sigmas = (0.05, 0.10, 0.20)
            threshs = (0.30, 0.50, 0.70)
            rand_count = 5
            run_count = 3
            max_starts = 14
        else:
            sigmas = (0.08, 0.18)
            threshs = (0.40, 0.60)
            rand_count = 2
            run_count = 1
            max_starts = 7

        starts = []
        starts.append(up)
        starts.append(random_balanced_binary(n, rng))
        for sg in sigmas:
            starts.append(up + rng.normal(0.0, float(sg), n))
        starts.append(run_based_init(n, rng))
        for tt in threshs:
            starts.append(binary_threshold(up, float(tt), n // 2, rng))
        for _ in range(rand_count):
            starts.append(random_balanced_binary(n, rng))
        for _ in range(max(0, run_count - 1)):
            starts.append(run_based_init(n, rng))

        starts = starts[:min(max_starts, len(starts))]
        total_starts = len(starts)

        for idx, st in enumerate(starts):
            if time.time() > binary_deadline - 12.0:
                break
            rem = binary_deadline - time.time()
            if rem <= 0.0:
                break
            left_now = max(1, total_starts - idx)
            if n <= 180:
                slot = max(8.0, min(35.0, rem / left_now))
            elif n <= 360:
                slot = max(12.0, min(70.0, rem / left_now))
            else:
                slot = max(18.0, min(120.0, rem / left_now))
            slot = min(slot, binary_deadline - time.time())
            if slot < 2.0:
                break

            try:
                h_b, c_b = optimize_binary_local(st, time.time() + slot, rng)
            except Exception:
                continue

            if not np.isfinite(c_b):
                continue

            if c_b < best_c - 1e-12:
                best_c = c_b
                best_h = h_b.astype(float)
                best_n = int(n)
            add_state(best_binary_states, c_b, h_b, n, 4)

    cont_deadline = max(deadline - 2.0, t0 + 30.0)
    cont_candidates = []

    if best_n <= 360:
        cont_candidates.append(np.asarray(best_h, dtype=float))
    else:
        cont_candidates.append(upsample_to(best_h, 360))

    if parent_n and parent_n <= 360:
        cont_candidates.append(parent.copy())

    for st in best_binary_states:
        c_st, h_st, n_st = st
        if n_st is None or n_st == 0:
            continue
        if c_st <= best_c + 1e-4:
            if n_st <= 360:
                cont_candidates.append(upsample_to(h_st.astype(float), n_st))
            else:
                cont_candidates.append(upsample_to(h_st.astype(float), 360))

    cont_candidates = cont_candidates[:4]
    for ci, cand in enumerate(cont_candidates):
        if time.time() > cont_deadline - 8.0:
            break
        rem = cont_deadline - time.time()
        if rem <= 0.0:
            break
        left_now = max(1, len(cont_candidates) - ci)
        if len(cand) <= 180:
            slot = max(15.0, min(90.0, rem / left_now))
        elif len(cand) <= 360:
            slot = max(25.0, min(160.0, rem / left_now))
        else:
            slot = max(40.0, min(240.0, rem / left_now))
        slot = min(slot, cont_deadline - time.time())
        if slot < 5.0:
            break

        try:
            h_c, c_c = run_slsqp_contin(cand, time.time() + slot)
        except Exception:
            continue

        if np.isfinite(c_c) and c_c < best_c - 1e-12:
            best_c = c_c
            best_h = h_c
            best_n = int(best_h.size)

    best_h = np.asarray(best_h, dtype=float)
    n = int(best_h.size)
    if n == 0:
        best_h = random_balanced_binary(180, rng).astype(float)
        n = 180

    if n % 2 == 0:
        h_round = binary_from_float(best_h, n // 2).astype(float)
        c_round = compute_c5(h_round)
        if np.isfinite(c_round) and c_round <= best_c + 1e-12:
            if c_round < best_c:
                best_c = c_round
            best_h = h_round
            target = n // 2
            if abs(float(best_h.sum()) - float(target)) > 1e-9:
                best_h = binary_from_float(best_h, target).astype(float)
    else:
        best_h = project_box_sum(best_h, n / 2.0)

    if not np.all(np.isfinite(best_h)):
        best_h = random_balanced_binary(180, rng).astype(float)
        n = 180

    best_h = np.clip(best_h, 0.0, 1.0)
    if n % 2 == 0:
        best_h = project_box_sum(best_h, n // 2)
    else:
        best_h = project_box_sum(best_h, n / 2.0)

    best_c = compute_c5(best_h)
    if not np.isfinite(best_c):
        best_h = random_balanced_binary(180, rng).astype(float)
        n = 180
        best_c = compute_c5(best_h)

    return best_h, float(best_c), int(best_h.size)
```
